Guides

Differences between a profile and a publication

Medium allows you to share your writing through two primary channels: your profile and publications. Here’s how they work and what sets them apart.

Profile

When you join Medium, you get your own profile page, which is linked to your account. This is your personal space where you can publish stories, customize your personal details, and manage your settings—only you have control over these features. Your profile address will be medium.com/@your-username. If you delete your account, your stories will also be deleted.

Publications

A publication works as a content hub, like a magazine. One person creates it and can invite others to join with different roles:

  • Writers can submit stories to the publication and edit their own content.
  • Editors can accept or reject submissions, edit any story in the publication, and publish their own stories. Stories published in a publication appear on both the author’s profile and the publication page. Publications offer more customization options than personal profiles, including different page layouts, newsletters, and the ability to create subpages. The publication address will be medium.com/publication-name.

Publications don’t have separate login credentials—they’re always managed through the owner’s and editors’ personal accounts and cannot be used to author stories. If a publication is deleted, all stories remain on their authors’ profiles, but the publication’s branding is removed.

Which one should you choose?

  • Use your profile if you want to build your personal brand and have full control over your content.
  • Join or create a publication if you want to reach Medium’s broader audience, collaborate with other writers, or need advanced customization features.

FAQ

I have a branded publication, and would like the stories published by the publication to be shown as authored by the brand, not by individual people. How do I do that?

If you’d like your stories to be authored by your organization or brand, you will need to create a user account under the brand’s name. You can use your brand’s name as your username and display name, and upload its logo as the profile picture.

Once you’ve set up the account, add it as a writer or editor in your existing publication, and use it to publish content in your brand’s name.

Can I delete the owner account and still keep the publication online?

No, you cannot. If you delete the user account which owns the publication, the publication will be taken offline.

Getting started with a Medium publication

This guide will tell you more about Medium publications and will walk you through the process of creating your own publication.

What is a Medium publication?

Medium publications are shared spaces for stories written around a common theme or topic, usually by multiple authors.

As such, publications can be created for many different purposes. Some example uses of Medium publications include:

  • An online magazine, a publication with a fixed publication staff that posts stories around a specific topic
  • A community publication, a publication that accepts stories published around Medium
  • A company blog, a publication that is created specifically to share company news
  • A collection of individual stories by a single author that are parts of a larger whole
Key features of Medium publications
  • Dedicated publication homepage with a customizable layout
  • Each publication has their own customizable publication homepage, where publication editors can feature stories published in their publication. In the publication’s settings, you can add a header with a title and/or logo, set up a number of sections with customizable layouts, and add feature pages which act as tabs housing specialized content.
  • Support for multiple contributors: writers and editors & story submission system
  • Each publication is owned by a single user account—the publication’s owner. The owner account has full rights to manage the publication and can add other users as publication editors and writers.
    • Editors can review, edit and publish or reject stories submitted by writers, as well as add their own stories, modify the publication’s layout, and add or remove publication writers.
    • Writers are regular contributors who can submit their stories to the publication.
  • Newsletter system
  • Publication editors can write and send custom email newsletters to the publication followers to connect with their readers and further build their audience.
  • Detailed stats page
  • Publications have a detailed stats page that allows publication editors to easily check how all the submitted stories are performing.
  • URL in the format: medium.com/your-publication-name

Who can create a Medium publication?

You must have a Medium account with an active membership subscription in order to create a Medium publication. Learn more about becoming a Medium member.

Where can I find a list of publications on Medium?

Medium doesn’t have an official list of publications, but this list is a good place to start. It also has links to each publication’s submission process (if they have one).

How to create a publication

  • On the web, click on your profile picture and choose Settings from the menu.
  • Click the Publishing tab or go to medium.com.
  • Click Manage publications.
  • Click Create a new publication.
  • Enter your publication’s name, description, and upload your publication avatar. These steps are required to create a publication.
  • Click Next in the lower right corner to go to the publication layout screen. Under the Homepage screen, you customize your publication’s homepage.
  • Click Create to create your publication. Once the publication is created, you can customize its layout, add editors and writers and submit stories to the publication.

You can learn more about all these features in this section of our Help Center.

FAQ

How many publications can I create?

Each Medium user can own up to seven Medium publications. There’s no limit of publications a user can participate in.

How to write a great newsletter

How do I set up a newsletter?

You must be a publication editor to set up a newsletter. Here’s how to create a publication and change the editors. If you want to get a head start and brush up on writing successful emails, read below — we’ve got you covered.

Define your purpose

  • The more clear and focused your newsletter’s purpose is the easier it will be for readers to connect with it, and share widely.
  • Here are some prompts to help get you started. Try to brainstorm at least one one of these:
    • Fill in this sentence. “I love this newsletter… I think you’d like it if you like it too if you like _____”
    • What will your reader learn, and why do they need that information?
    • What can your newsletter provide that others can’t? Do your editors have a specific expertise, voice, or following that readers respond to?
    • What kind of habit do you want to build? How often does your reader need or want this information?

Connect with your readers

  • Talk directly to your reader as you would a friend or trusted colleague.
  • Be genuine and approachable, and let your personality shine. If your newsletter and publication are not highly specialized, avoid jargon.
  • Engage with your readers by asking them to reply to your newsletter with questions.
  • Thank them for reading.

Make your email scannable

  • Break it up into sections using font size and line breaks, remember that a lot of people read email on their phones.
  • Bold or italicize for emphasis. Use this sparingly and only where important to really draw the reader’s eye in. Overdoing stylized fonts can make the email feel overwhelming.
  • Include links to your posts, make it easier on the reader by hyperlinking towards the top of your email.
  • Consider including a “reading list” at the end of the email to make finding links quicker. Here, you may also want to include links to reading from across the web that’s relevant to the content of your newsletter.

Craft a great subject line

  • 60 characters or less
  • Use sentence case so that not all the words are capitalized.
  • Target a distinct emotion that will help people connect to the email. Usually evoking curiosity in the subject line and satisfying it in the email is a successful tactic.
  • Experiment with emojis to help your subject line stand out. You can copy/paste from emojipedia or use the shortcut command+control+spacebar to have the emojis pop up on Mac.

How to grow your newsletter audience

  • Include a line in your newsletter encouraging your readers to forward the email to friends who share similar interests.
  • Include your newsletter sign up page in your publication homepage navigation, so that your readers can easily find it.
  • Share widely with your circle of friends, family, and colleagues.
  • Make your newsletter sign up page easy to find on your social media accounts. Include it in your bio, a pinned tweet, link to it directly from your posts.
  • If you are using the beta publication tools, on your Newsletter settings page, click the checkmark for “Promote your newsletter on your publication’s stories” to show your newsletter signup form at the bottom of your stories.

Medium Publications Best Practices and Guidelines (Guides)

Medium publications are collections of stories about a common theme or topic. They function as thriving editorial communities that connect writers with readers and to help great ideas find the audiences they deserve.

Please read the following guidelines for publications for tips on managing your publication.

Best practices for owners and editors

Adding editors and writers

Publications owners can add editors and writers to their staff; publications editors can add writers. This is a powerful tool for building your editorial community, but it should be used thoughtfully.

Medium offers an “open submissions” feature, which allows any writer to submit a story to your publication. We encourage you to use open submissions as your main way to bring in new writers.

Please do not add people in bulk or without a genuine editorial purpose, particularly to inflate a publication’s apparent size or reach. Adding people without additional communication is usually experienced as a form of spam.

Editing and collaboration

Publications are meant to be collaborative. Editors and writers have equal editing permissions, and we encourage them to use our private notes system to communicate. The best publications combine the strengths of writers and editors to improve every story.

Do not use editing permissions in ways that harm the writer’s work. This includes: making major changes to a story without the writer’s input, adding large footers or calls to action, removing a story from the paywall, or deleting a story without agreement from the writer.

Mentions and solicitation

At-mentions in a story are an effective way to communicate with your community of writers and highlight certain groups of contributors.

Responses and private notes are appropriate ways to reach out to writers whose work might be a good fit for your publication.

Please do not abuse mentions by mentioning uninvolved accounts or sharing at-mention lists. If someone asks you to stop mentioning them, respect that request. Do not use responses or private notes to contact writers in bulk or without a specific reason.

Friend Links

Publication owners and editors can create Friend Links for stories in their publications. Friend Links bypass the paywall and are meant to be shared with readers who don’t have a Medium membership. They are a good way to grow your audience — readers who find great content this way may become members, which benefits both writers and publications.

Do not use Friend Links in ways that interfere with a writer’s distribution choices or earnings—including posting them directly in the story itself.

Best practices for writers

Submitting to publications

Medium’s open submissions system makes it easy to find publications that are a good fit for your work and submit stories directly for consideration. Before submitting, take the time to find the publication that best matches the topic and tone of your story, and put your best work forward. Editors are more likely to accept stories that are a natural fit for their publication.

If a publication is closed to submissions, we ask that you respect that. Look for the publication’s guidelines and use the process they outline to ask to contribute.

Platform rules

Copyright and content licensing

As stated in our Terms of Service, Medium authors grant Medium a nonexclusive, royalty-free, worldwide, fully paid, and sublicensable license to display their content across Medium.com surfaces, including Publications on Medium. That license does not extend to Publication owners, editors, or any other staff.

If you move your publication to another website or platform, you may not republish, copy, or otherwise reproduce any stories from your publication there without the express permission of each story’s author. More broadly, you may not export, display, syndicate, or distribute content from your Publication on any non-Medium surface without clear permission from the copyright owner. You may not require authors to grant you any of these permissions in order to submit to or participate in your publication.

Third-party advertising

Medium does not allow you to include third-party advertising or sponsorships in stories. You may not advertise or promote third-party products, services, or brands through Medium posts, publications, or newsletters. This includes images that indicate brand sponsorship in a post, newsletter, or any element of your publication’s homepage, or as part of a publication name or logo. First-party advertising—that which is for your own product, brand, mailing list, event, or similar promotion—is allowed.

Additional Terms of Service or Policies

All Medium users accept the Medium Terms of Service. You may not require authors to agree to any additional Terms of Service to submit or participate in your publication. You may create submission criteria or content requirements that do not constitute Terms of Service.

Sale of Publication

You may not sell your publication. If you wish to transfer ownership of your publication to another Medium user, please contact our Support team.

Custom domains

Publication owners are able to attach a custom domain to their publication. Custom domains are intended to give your publication a distinct identity while remaining fully part of the Medium platform. If you point your custom domain away from your publication, we will disconnect it from Medium.

When operating a custom domain, you may not:

  • Alter the look and feel or transform the content of the webpage, including by injecting ads, inserting tracking code, or altering the Medium look or feel;
  • Use proxy servers or any other means to short-circuit our rules or circumvent user protections;
  • Mislead users about what site they are on or what their actions will do; or
  • Manipulate SEO or domain authority, including by altering or redirecting canonical URLs away from any URL generated by the author or medium.com.

Editor Partner Program

The Editor Partner Program allows editors of qualifying publications to earn from the stories they edit.

Editors may not earn by editing their own stories. This includes assigning stories to alternate accounts or any other account over which you have full or partial control. Creating additional writer accounts for the purpose of editing and claiming your own stories is a violation of the Editor Partner Program and Partner Program Policy.

For writers and editors both, purchasing external traffic and directing it at paywalled stories in your publication is not something we recommend. It won’t improve your earnings or distribution in any meaningful way, and low-quality traffic can actively harm your publication’s standing on Medium and in search.

Publishing on Medium as a company

You have two main options for publishing on Medium as a company, brand, or organization: publish from a regular user account under your organization’s name, or set up a publication and have multiple people in your organization write as themselves and publish in that publication. Or you can do both.

Each user account has a profile page that is visible to everyone and can be used as your public persona on Medium. Most brands create a general user account for their organization in the same way they do on other social media sites. User accounts are great for publishing things that the organization as a whole (rather than a specific individual) has to say—corporate communications, announcements, and the like.

Publications allow you to grow your brand’s presence on Medium beyond a single account. Publications are essentially content hubs that allow stories published by many authors to live in one place. Each publication is owned by a specific user account, but can publish stories submitted by multiple accounts. As the publication’s owner, you’ll be able to customize the layout of its homepage and add subpages (tabs) for specialized content. The publication will live under medium.com/your-publication-name.

To learn more about publications and how to create one, see this article.

Tips for promoting your newsletter sign-up page

Once your Newsletter is set up, click Newsletter in the Manage publication menu on your publication homepage. This URL is your sign-up page. This page is dynamic and will show sign-up buttons for anyone who is not an editor of the publication.

Here are some tips that can help you promote your newsletter sign-up page:

  • Include a line in your newsletter encouraging your readers to forward the email to friends who share similar interests.
  • Include your newsletter sign-up page in your publication homepage navigation, so that your readers can easily find it.
  • Share widely with your circle of friends, family, and colleagues.
  • Make your newsletter sign-up page easy to find on your social media accounts. Include it in your bio, a pinned tweet, and link to it directly from your posts. Please note: Readers can subscribe to your Newsletter separately from following your publication. Readers can subscribe to your Newsletter on its landing page.

Learn more about setting up your newsletter or discover tips for crafting a great one.

Homepage

Create and manage lists

Lists are an easy way to quickly organize and share stories, from personal favorites to recommended reads within a topic or area of expertise to themed lists around stories that inspire you.

Adding a story to a list

Desktop

  • When reading a story, click the Save button.
  • The story by default will be added to the Reading list. Select all the lists you want to add the story to. To remove the story from a list, deselect it.

Saving a list to your library

Desktop

  • Open a public list that you want to add to your library.
  • Click the Save list button. Your list will appear in the Saved lists tab.

Creating a new list

Desktop iOS & Android

  • On the homepage sidebar, click Library.
  • On the Library page, click the New list button to create a new list.
  • Enter the name for your list and description (optional).
  • Choose to make your list public or private.
    • Public lists can be shared with anyone and are visible on your profile page under the Lists tab.
    • Private lists cannot be accessed by anyone but you.
  • Click Create to create your list.
  • In the app, tap the Library button on the bottom bar.
  • On the Library page, tap the New list button to create a new list.
  • Enter the name for your list and description (optional).
  • Choose to make your list public or private.
    • Public lists can be shared with anyone and are visible on your profile page under the Lists tab.
    • Private lists cannot be accessed by anyone but you.
  • Tap Create to create your list.

Managing your lists

Desktop iOS & Android

  • On the homepage sidebar, click Library.
  • Click the list you want to manage to open it.
  • On the list page, you can:
    • Click the three-dot button to Edit list info, make it private or public, reorder stories, or delete your list.
    • Add a note to appear above each story on the list.
    • Remove stories from your lists, or add them to your other lists.
    • Click the Share button to share your list on social media or click Copy link to copy the direct link to the list.
  • In the app, tap the Library button on the bottom bar.
  • Tap the list you want to manage to open it.
  • On the list page, you can:
    • Tap the three-dot button to edit list info, make it private or public, reorder stories, share the list with your friends, or delete your list.
    • Add a note to appear above each story on the list.
    • Remove stories from your lists, or add them to your other lists.
    • Tap the Share button to share your list on social media or click Copy link to copy the direct link to the list.
    • Tap the Download button to download the list for offline reading. Tap the button again to remove the downloaded content. Please note: “Reading list” is your default list which cannot be deleted.

Leaving responses

If you have a public list, other Medium users are able to leave responses on your list.

Web App

  • Click the Responses button at the bottom of the list page to open the response panel on the right side.
  • Click What are your thoughts? to start composing your response.
  • Click Respond to publish your response.
  • Tap the Responses button at the bottom of the list page.
  • Tap Write a response open the editor and start composing your response.

Managing responses

As the list creator, you have the ability to hide unwanted responses or close the response section so no further responses can be posted. Learn more.

Follow writers and publications

You can follow writers and publications to see what they publish on your feeds.

In addition to that, you can optionally enable notifications for your preferred writers to receive an email whenever they publish a story and choose to notify their audiences.

Following a writer

To follow a writer, you just need to click the Follow button next to them.

To get email notifications about stories from a writer you’d like to follow:

  • Click the Follow button
  • Select Email notifications on from the dropdown menu When that writer publishes a new story and chooses to send it to their email subscribers, you’ll receive a story preview directly to your inbox.

To adjust email notifications about stories from writers you already follow:

  • Click the Following button
  • Select your notification preference from the dropdown menu Note: Your email won’t be shared with writers when you enable the email notifications.

Following a publication

Similar to the writer, click the Follow button next to the publication to follow it.

Mute a writer or publication

Desktop iOS & Android

From your feeds

  • On your feeds, click the three-dot button on the story preview.
  • Click Mute this author to mute the writer, or Mute this publication to mute the publication the story is published in.

From a profile

  • Go to the profile page that you want to mute and click the three-dot button in the main area.
  • Click Mute this author to mute the writer.

From your feeds

  • On your feeds, tap the three-dot button on the story preview.
  • Tap Mute this author to mute the writer, or Mute this publication to mute the publication the story is published in.

From a profile

  • Go to the profile page that you want to mute and tap the three-dot button in the top-right corner.
  • Tap Mute this author to mute the writer.

What happens when you mute a writer or publication?

  • All existing stories from the writer or publication you muted will be removed from your feed when you refresh the page or relaunch the app.
  • You won’t see any of their stories on your homepage or in email digests.
  • You can still follow the writer and publication you muted, as well as view their profile page or publication homepage, but their content won’t be recommended to you in your feeds.

Managing muted accounts and publications

Desktop iOS & Android

  • On your homepage, click on your profile picture to open the menu.
  • Click Settings.
  • Click Muted writers and publications or go to medium.com/me/settings/mute.
  • In the app, tap your profile picture on the bottom bar.
  • Tap Settings in the top-right corner.
  • Tap Account.
  • Tap Muted writers and publications.

Refine recommendations

By following topics, writers, and publications that match your interests, you will receive better story recommendations on your homepage.

Web App

  • On your homepage sidebar, click the Following button.
  • On your following feed, click the plus button.
  • On the Refine recommendations page, you can:
    • Follow recommended topics, publications, and writers in the Suggestions tab.
    • Manage the topics, publications, and authors you already follow in the Following tab.
    • Check your reading history in the Reading history tab.
    • Check your muted authors and publications in the Muted tab.

Reading history

The reading history section lets you check your reading history and remove stories from your history to adjust your recommendations. You can also mute writers and publications.

You can also clear your reading history to reset your recommendations altogether.

Muted

The mute section lets you check the writers and publications you mute and remove them. Learn more about muting.

  • In the app, tap your profile picture on the bottom bar.
  • On your homepage, click on your profile picture and click Settings.
  • Tap Refine recommendations.

Show less like this

Use this feature to hide a story from your feed and to signal what stories you would like to see less frequently on your feed.

Desktop

  • On your feed, press the minus button near the story preview.

Following and unfollowing specific authors and publications

Medium also lets you follow specific writers and publications. Once you follow a writer or a publication, stories published by them will show up on your homepage and in your email digests.

Web App

Follow or unfollow a writer

To follow a writer on Medium:

  • Go to the profile page of the user you wish to follow.
  • Click Follow. To unfollow a writer, click the Following button on their profile page.
Follow or unfollow a publication

To follow a publication on Medium:

  • Go to the publication homepage of the publication you wish to follow.
  • Click Follow. To unfollow a publication, click the Following button on their publication homepage, and choose Unfollow publication from the menu.
Follow or unfollow a writer

To follow a writer on Medium:

  • Go to the profile page of the user you wish to follow.
  • Tap Follow. To unfollow a writer, tap the Following button on their profile page.
Follow or unfollow a publication

To follow a publication on Medium:

  • Go to the publication homepage of the publication you wish to follow.
  • Tap Follow. To unfollow a publication, tap the Following button on their publication homepage.

Your homepage

The Medium homepage is the place to go to quickly see the latest from your favorite writers and publications on the topics that matter to you most.

On your homepage, you can navigate to different feeds:

  • For you: Top stories based on your personalized recommendations.

  • Featured: Featured stories from publications you follow. On the right side of the homepage, you can also find:

  • Staff picks: Curated stories recommended by our staff.

  • Recommended topics: Topics we think might interest you.

  • Who to follow: Popular authors we think you might like.

  • Reading list: Latest stories that you’ve added to your Reading list.

Following feed

On your homepage sidebar, click Following.

On this page, you can find all the stories published by the authors and publications you follow in reverse chronological order. Click Topics to browse through the latest stories in your followed topics. To follow more writers, publications, and topics, click the plus button.

Customizing your homepage

You can control what stories appear on your Medium homepage by:

  • Following topics that you are interested in.
  • Following writers and publications from their profile pages and publication homepages.
  • Muting stories from writers and publications you don’t want to see stories from.
Search

You can also search Medium stories, accounts, and publications using the search feature.

Web App

  • Enter your search query in the search input in the top-left corner of the page.
  • Click on the suggestions or tap Enter to see all the results.
  • In the app, tap the magnifying glassicon on the bottom bar.
  • Enter your search query and tap Search.

Login

About passwords

At this time, Medium does not support a password system for signing in to your account.

Instead, you can sign in to your Medium account using your email address or a social media account that you’ve connected with your Medium account.

Sign in or sign up with a social media account

Desktop iOS & Android

  • On the Medium homepage, click Sign in in the top-right corner to sign in to your existing Medium account, or Get started to create a new account.
  • Choose the third-party service you want to use.
  • Authorize Medium to access your social media account.
  • Click Ok to be redirected to Medium and signed in to your account.
  • Open the Medium app on your device.
  • Choose the third-party service you want to use.
  • Authorize Medium to access your social account.
  • Click Ok to be redirected to Medium and signed in to your account.

Sign in or sign up with email

Desktop iOS & Android

  • On the Medium homepage, click Sign in in the top-right corner.

  • Choose Sign in with email.

  • Enter your email address and click Continue.

  • Go to your inbox and open the verification email.

  • Click Sign in to Medium in the email to be redirected to Medium and signed in to your account. Note: The email verification link expires after two hours and can only be used once. If you run out of time or need a new email login later on, you can simply follow the same process again to get a fresh email login link.

  • Open the Medium app on your device.

  • Tap Sign in with email.

  • Enter your email address and tap Continue.

  • Go to your inbox on your device device and open the verification email. You must open the verification link from the same device you requested it.

  • Tap Sign in to Medium in the email to be redirected to Medium and signed in to your account. Note: The email verification link expires after two hours and can only be used once. If you run out of time or need a new email login later on, you can simply follow the same process again to get a fresh email login link.

Troubleshooting

I don’t know how to access my account

If you don’t know what social media account is associated with your Medium account, try signing in using your email address instead, and when you sign in, check your social connections in Settings.

If you don’t know what email address you created your account with, check your email inbox. You should have received a welcome email from Medium when you created your account for the first time.

I’m not receiving verification emails from Medium

Please see this article for more information.

Can I set up a password?

Medium does not support passwords at this time.

Sharing account access

If you’re trying to set up a shared Medium account that multiple people from the same organization can sign into, we recommend setting up a shared-access email account in your company domain or a shared Twitter account linked to your Medium account. This would allow anyone with access to the Twitter account to sign into the linked Medium account.

Likewise, anyone with access to a shared email account can receive Medium’s email sign in link (valid for 2 hours) to access that account. Please note - If you are forwarding the sign-in email to a group inbox that may expire the sign-in link.

Please note that our Paid Terms of Service do not allow for sharing a single Medium account to provide membership access to multiple people. You can only share accounts for publishing purposes.

Trouble receiving emails from Medium

If you’re having problems with receiving emails from Medium, including sign in links or email digests, this article is for you! We’ve gathered some tips to help you troubleshoot this problem.

Step 1: Make sure your email address is correct

DesktopiOS & Android

  • On your homepage, click on your profile picture and click Settings.
  • Under Your email, double check if your email address is correct. If you need to update it, click Edit email and confirm the change by clicking Save.
  • In the app, tap your profile picture on the bottom bar.
  • Tap Settings in the top-right corner.
  • Tap Email notifications.
  • Under Your email, double check if your email address is correct. If you need to update it, click Edit email and confirm the change by clicking Save.

Step 2: Check your spam, trash folders, and other email filters

Search your “All Mail” and “Spam” inbox for emails from Medium. If you set up any custom email filters, double check them to see if they send Medium emails into some other folder.

Step 3: Allow receiving emails from Medium

GmailYahooHotmailOutlookOther

  • On your computer, go to Google Contacts.

  • In the bottom right, click Create contact.

  • Enter the email address noreply@medium.com.

  • Click Save. If you have an email from Medium in your inbox, you can also hover over the senders name and click Add to contacts from there.

  • Log in to your Yahoo email inbox, hover over the Settings icon and click Settings.

  • In the Settings tab, select Filters from the left menu and click the button to Add a filter.

  • Type in a name for your filter. Select “contains” in the “sender” drop-down menu, and type in noreply@medium.com in the “sender” text field. In the “Move to folder” drop-down menu, select “Inbox”.

  • Click Save in the top bar to save your filter.

  • Log in to your Hotmail email inbox. In the top-right corner, click the Settings icon.

  • Click Options.

  • Click Safe and blocked senders.

  • Click Safe senders.

  • Enter the email address noreply@medium.com and click Add to list.

  • Right-click on the email message in your inbox.

  • Select Junk E-mail from the menu.

  • Click Add Sender to Safe Senders List. AOL

  • Log in to your AOL email inbox. In the top-right corner, click Options and then Mail Settings.

  • In the Settings page, click Spam Settings.

  • Choose Allow mail only from addresses I specify from the drop-down menu.

  • Enter the email address noreply@medium.com and click the plus button.

  • Click Save to save your changes. Verizon

  • Log in to your Verizon email inbox.

  • Click Options.

  • Click Block Senders.

  • Locate the Safe List.

  • Enter the email address noreply@medium.com.

  • Click OK. Comcast

  • Log in to your Comcast email inbox and click Address Book.

  • Click New and click New Contact.

  • Add email address noreply@medium.com.

  • Click Save. If you’re still not receiving emails from Medium, you might also ask your IT team, email administrator, or ISP if your email server is blocking emails from Medium. Emails from Medium come from noreply@medium.com, so that email address might need to be whitelisted on the email server level.

Get help

If you still have problems with receiving emails, please contact Medium Support for further assistance.

Trouble signing in or staying signed in to Medium

If you’re having problems signing up, signing in, or you’re frequently being signed out of your Medium account, we’ve gathered several tips that might help you troubleshoot the problem. Please read and try all of the troubleshooting items listed on this page prior to contacting Medium Support.

Clear your browser cookies

Google Chrome Safari FirefoxEdge

  • Click the More button in the top-right corner of your browser window.
  • Choose Settings from the menu.
  • Under Privacy and security, click Site settings.
  • Click Cookies and site data.
  • Click See all cookies and site data.
  • Choose medium.com from the list.
  • Click Remove all.
  • Choose Safari and click Preferences.
  • Click Privacy.
  • Click Manage Website Data.
  • Select medium.com from the list
  • Click Remove All.
  • Go to medium.com and click the padlock button to the left of the address bar.
  • Click Clear cookies and site data.
  • Click the More button in the top-right corner of your browser window.
  • Choose Settings from the menu.
  • Click Cookies and site permissions in the side menu.
  • Click Manage and delete cookies and site data.
  • Click See all cookies and site data.
  • Enter medium.com in the search box.
  • Click Remove all shown. If you use another browser, please refer to your browser’s support pages.

If you’ve cleared your browser cookies and the issue persists, try also clearing any cookies related to the custom domain publications, e.g. towardsdatascience.com, that you are landing on and getting logged out.

Make sure third-party cookies are enabled

If your third-party cookies are turned off, you might also experience problems while browsing Medium.

Google Chrome Safari FirefoxEdge

  • Click the More button in the top-right corner of your browser window.
  • Choose Settings from the menu.
  • Under Privacy and security, click Site settings.
  • Click Cookies and site data.
  • In the Cookies section, make sure Block third-party cookies is not on. The radio button should not be filled in.
  • Choose Safari and click Preferences.
  • Click Privacy.
  • In Website tracking: If “Ask websites not to track me” is checked, or if “Prevent cross-site tracking” is checked, un-check them.
  • Click the menu button and select Content blocking.
  • In the Privacy & Security panel of Firefox Settings, do one of the following:
    • Select Standard to restore all default cookies settings for your browser.
    • Select Custom and clear the cookies check mark.
  • Click the More button in the top-right corner of your browser window.
  • Choose Settings from the menu.
  • Click Cookies and site permissions in the side menu.
  • Click Manage and delete cookies and site data.
  • Make sure Block third-party cookies is turned off. The toggle should be gray.

Make sure your browser extensions don’t interfere with Medium

Browser extensions that block paywalls will prevent you from signing in to Medium. Disable these extensions if you want to use Medium.

If you use an ad blocking extension on your browser, you also might need to add exceptions for the domains on Medium that you visit most, otherwise, you might still get logged out. You can find instructions for adding exceptions to ad blockers here.

Trouble signing in to Medium using Sign in with Apple

To sign in to an existing Medium account using “Sign in with Apple,” the email address associated with your Apple ID MUST match the email address associated with the Medium account you’re trying to sign in to. You will NOT be able to sign in to an existing Medium account if you opt to hide your email address during the Sign in with Apple flow.

There are 2 ways to resolve this issue:

Sign in to your Medium account using the method you used to sign up, and do not use “Sign in with Apple.”

OR

Choose to “share your email address” during the Sign in with Apple flow. If your Apple ID email address matches the email address on your Medium account, it will connect automatically. You will then be able to use Sign in with Apple going forward.

To do this, follow the steps below:

  • Open your iOS Settings app
  • Select the top option where your name is displayed with Apple ID, iCloud, iTunes & Ap… beneath your name
  • Select Password & Security
  • Select Apps Using Your Apple ID
  • Select Medium
  • Select Stop Using Apple ID
  • Go back to the Medium app on your device
  • Select Sign in with Apple
  • Select Share My Email

Trouble with accessing my Medium account

If you’re having problems with signing in to your Medium account, this guide is for you.

Troubleshooting

Step 1: Find your Medium account

Make sure that you know what account you have created on Medium. You can find your Medium account in multiple ways:

  • Check if you can find any other emails from Medium in your email inboxes. If you have received emails from Medium to a specific inbox, it means that you have an account associated with that email address.
  • If you have a membership subscription, check all your email inboxes and try to find the email titled “Thank you for becoming a member”. If you are able to find that email, it means that your membership Medium account is associated with that email address.
Step 2: Sign in to your primary account

If you know which of your email addresses is associated with your primary Medium account, you will need to sign out of your existing account and sign in to your primary account instead.

Desktop iOS & Android

  • On the Medium homepage, click Sign in in the top-right corner.

  • Choose Sign in with email.

  • Enter your email address and click Continue.

  • Go to your inbox and open the verification email.

  • Click Sign in to Medium in the email to be redirected to Medium and signed in to your account. Note: The email verification link expires after two hours and can only be used once. If you run out of time or need a new email login later on, you can simply follow the same process again to get a fresh email login link.

  • Open the Medium app on your device.

  • Tap Sign in with email.

  • Enter your email address and tap Continue.

  • Go to your inbox on your device device and open the verification email. You must open the verification link from the same device you requested it.

  • Tap Sign in to Medium in the email to be redirected to Medium and signed in to your account. Note: The email verification link expires after two hours and can only be used once. If you run out of time or need a new email login later on, you can simply follow the same process again to get a fresh email login link.

Check your social connections

In your Settings page, you can check whether you have any social accounts associated with your Medium account. If you’ve previously connected Twitter, Facebook, or Google to your account, you can also use those social media accounts to sign in to your Medium account. You can connect and disconnect your social accounts at any time.

Trouble with merging duplicate Medium accounts

If you’ve accidentally created multiple accounts on Medium, this guide can help you remove all your additional accounts and keep your primary Medium account.

Medium currently does not offer a way to merge multiple Medium accounts or migrate account data (stories, bookmarks, membership subscription, followers, etc.) between accounts. Instead, you can manually move all the relevant data between your accounts, and then delete your inactive accounts.

Troubleshooting

Step 1: Locate all your Medium accounts

Make sure that you know what are the accounts that you have created on Medium and how to access them.

If you accidentally signed in to one of your other Medium accounts, go to your account Settings page to find your username and email address associated with that account. If at this point you’re able to confirm that this account is not your primary account, you can delete it right away from the bottom of the Settings page.

It’s also recommended to check all your email inboxes to see if you have any emails from Medium. If you do, it means that there’s an account associated with that email address.

You might also find your other accounts by searching for your name on medium.com.

Step 2: Manually move your data between accounts

If you find that you’ve been accidentally using multiple Medium accounts at the same time (e.g. from multiple devices), you might want to manually move some of your data between those accounts before you decide to delete one.

Please note: Medium Support does not have the ability to move your account data (such as your followers/following, bookmarks, drafts, reading history, claps, highlights, your membership subscription, your Partner Program information, and more) between accounts.

In that case, you might want to check your secondary account to see if you can manually move something to your main account:

  • Go to your bookmarks to see if you have any stories that you might want to bookmark on your primary account
  • Check the accounts you follow to see if there’s someone you might want to follow from your primary account
  • Check your Stories page to see if you have any published stories or responses
  • Check if you have any drafts that you can copy-paste to your primary account
  • Check your Settings page to see if you have a membership subscription attached to that account. Your membership subscription

If you have a membership subscription on one of your accounts, you might want to keep that account. Otherwise, you would need to cancel your subscription on that account and re-subscribe from the account that you wish to keep.

Step 3: Delete your inactive Medium account(s)

Make sure you’re currently signed in to the account you wish to delete and follow the instructions below.

Desktop iOS & Android

Note: This deletion is permanent, and all your account data will be removed in accordance with our Privacy Policy.

  • On your homepage, click on your profile picture in the top-right corner and click Settings.

  • Click Delete account at the bottom of the page.

  • Confirm the action by entering your username. Note: This deletion is permanent, and all your account data will be removed in accordance with our Privacy Policy.

  • In the app, tap your profile picture on the bottom bar.

  • Tap Settings in the top-right corner.

  • Tap Account

  • Tap Delete account at the bottom of the page.

  • Confirm the action by typing “delete” in English

If you accidentally created multiple Medium accounts, this guide will help you sign in to your primary Medium account.

You might have multiple Medium accounts if you:

  • became a Medium member but still don’t have access to members-only content
  • lost access to your published stories and drafts on Medium or other account data Duplicate accounts might be inadvertently created when you try to sign in to an existing Medium account using an email address or a social media account that is not associated with your original Medium account. When that happens, Medium automatically prompts you to create a new account instead.

  • Check if you can find any other emails from Medium in your email inboxes. If you have received emails from Medium to a specific inbox, it means that you have an account associated with that email address.
Step 2: Sign in to your primary account

If you know which of your email addresses is associated with your primary Medium account, you will need to sign out of your existing account and sign in to your primary account instead.

DesktopiOS & Android

  • On your homepage, click on your profile picture.

  • Click Sign out.

  • On the Medium homepage, click Sign in in the top-right corner.

  • Choose Sign in with email.

  • Enter your email address and click Continue.

  • Go to your inbox and open the verification email.

  • Click Sign in to Medium in the email to be redirected to Medium and signed in to your account. Note: The email verification link expires after two hours and can only be used once. If you run out of time or need a new email login later on, you can simply follow the same process again to get a fresh email login link.

  • In the app, tap your profile picture on the bottom bar.

  • Tap Settings in the top-right corner.

  • Tap Account and payment.

  • Tap Sign out.

  • Open the Medium app on your device.

  • Tap Sign in with email.

  • Enter your email address and tap Continue.

  • Go to your inbox on your device device and open the verification email. You must open the verification link from the same device you requested it.

  • Tap Sign in to Medium in the email to be redirected to Medium and signed in to your account. Note: The email verification link expires after two hours and can only be used once. If you run out of time or need a new email login later on, you can simply follow the same process again to get a fresh email login link.

Managing stories

About Friend Links

About Friend Links shared by Authors and Pub Editors

Friend Links are a tool for writers to guarantee that friends, family, and fans have free access to their paywalled stories.

When your story is behind the paywall, only Medium members can read it in its entirety (non-members will be able to read the opening section of your story). People without Medium accounts who land on your story will be prompted to create a free Medium account and will be able to read the story they were on when they created their free account. After that, they will need to become a Medium member to read additional paywalled stories.

If your story is not behind our paywall, it’s already free for all to read, and you can share it using your story URL.

We understand there are moments where you want to make sure friends, family, and fans can read your work no matter what, so we created a handy Friend Link that gives anyone free access to your story — even if they’re not a subscribing Medium member.

Authors receive Friend Links for every story that’s behind our paywall. You can share a Friend Link directly to, well, friends or through any social media platform.

Sharing a Friend Link

Desktop iOS & Android

  • Open the story for which you want to grab the Friend Link.
  • Click on the share icon in the story menu.
  • Choose Copy Friend Link from the menu.
  • Copy your custom Friend Link and share away! Anyone who uses this link will have free access.
  • In the app, tap on the share icon at the top right.
  • Tap Friend Link on the bottom menu.
  • Select sharing method.

Common questions about Friend Links

Who can I share a Friend Link with?

You can share a Friend Link with anyone. As long as they have that link, they’ll have free access. And people can share it with other people too — it doesn’t just have to come from you. You will need to share the original Friend Link with others first in order for people to be able to access and share that link with others, however — only the author can see the Friend Link when viewing their stories.

Will sharing a Friend Link impact my earnings?

No – if you share your paywalled story via a Friend Link, you will continue to earn for engagement from paying members who access it via your Friend Link.

Can I get a Friend Link if I publish in a publication?

Yes, the author of the story and a publication editor can generate a Friend Link for stories in publications. The link is in the top right corner for posts in publications.

About Friend Links shared by a Friend of Medium

Friends of Medium are members who pay more money for their membership in order to give more support to Medium and the writers they love and read. Reads generated by Friends generate 4x more earnings for writers. Being a Friend of Medium also allows them to share Friend Links with their network and support writers even further.

Friend Links shared by Friends of Medium bypass the paywall and pay writers for reading engagement, even if those readers are not Medium members.

I’m a Friend of Medium, what does this mean for me?

First of all, thank you for being a Friend of Medium! We know that supporting writers is important to you and we’re excited that you’re interested in sharing their stories with your world. You can find your Friend Link on an article by going to the Share button and selecting Copy Friend Link. From there, you can share away!

What if I was a Friend of Medium, but I no longer am?

If you are no longer a Friend of Medium, any Friend Links you have shared in the past will show a paywall to ensure that writers are still paid for reading engagement on their story. You will be unable to share new Friend Links until you upgrade your account to become a Friend of Medium once again. This also applies to Friends of Medium who delete their account or are suspended.

I’m a Partner Program writer, what does this mean for me?

You may (or may not!) know that writers have had Friend Links for quite some time in order to share your paywalled stories with your friends, families, and fans. Friend Links shared by authors or publication editors will work the exact same way, meaning that reads to these links will not generate revenue.

Please note this is true even if you upgrade to become a Friend of Medium. This is similar to how things work now, you cannot generate revenue for yourself by reading your own story (or sharing your own Friend Link).

Reads from Friend Links will show up on the Post Stats Page. They will be separated by Friend Links shared by you and Friend Links shared by a member with a Friend membership. You are paid for the reads from Friend Links shared by Friends of Medium.

Common questions about Friend Links shared by a Friend of Medium

Who can I share a Friend Link with?

Friends of Medium can share a Friend Link with anyone. As long as they have that link, and you remain a Friend of Medium, they’ll have access without a paywall. And people can share it with other people too — it doesn’t just have to come from you. You will need to hare the original Friend Link with others first in order for people to be able to access and share that link with others. However, only Friends of Medium and authors can see the Friend Lin when viewing stories.

Will sharing a Friend Link as a Friend of Medium impact writer earnings?

Yes! As a Friend of Medium, if you share a story via a Friend Link, the writer will earn for engagement from non-members who access your Friend Link. The writer will also continue to earn for engagement from paying members and Friends who access the story via your Friend Link.

Is the Friend of Medium Friend Link different from the author of publication editor’s Friend Link?

Yes, Friend of Medium Friend Links are unique to each Friend of Medium. Author and publication editor friend links are also unique. Only Friend of Medium Friend Links earn money for writers for non-reading engagement.

What if I suspect abuse?

We regularly monitor traffic to Friend Links to ensure that no one is taking advantage of our system. If you suspect a Friend of Medium is abusing Friend Links, please reach out to our Trust and Safety team here. Friend Links are a privilege, not a right. 🙏

About shortform content

Shortform on Medium is a post that is 150 words or less. This excludes kickers, titles, subtitles, and image captions.

If you write a post that is 150 words or less, Medium will automatically classify that as shortform content. (You can check your word count by highlighting all the text in your draft or edit mode (excluding kicker, title, subtitle, or image caption), and the word count will appear in the top left corner.)

Distribution of shortform content

  • Shortform content only appears on profile and beta publication feeds.
  • It is not eligible for additional curated distribution at this time.
  • It will not fan out to your followers’ Daily Digests at this time.

Audience stats

You can track the growth of your audience on your Audience stats page.

Web App

  • To access your Stats, click on the menu icon in the top left corner to open the sidebar.

  • Click on Stats.

  • Click on Audience, or go to medium.com.

  • To access your Stats, tap on your profile picture in the bottom right corner.

  • Tap View Stats.

  • Tap Audience. Audience is defined as follows:

  • Followers: Readers who follow you on Medium. This excludes deactivated, deleted, or suspended users.

  • Subscribers: Readers who have opted to receive emails from you. This excludes unsubscribed, deactivated, deleted, or suspended users. This page is updated daily and displays your current total followers and email subscribers in the Lifetime section.

In the Audience growth section, you can see graphs that show you how your audience has changed month over month. You can also review previous years by clicking on the pill-shaped select and selecting the year to review, or download a CSV with your audience stats.

Please note: Due to the frequency of updates made to the data on this page, you may notice that the number of followers may differ on other pages. To see your most up-to-date count of followers, visit your profile page.

Subscriber list

You can see who your subscribers are by clicking on View subscriber list. Here you can view their name and subscription date. The list excludes those who have unsubscribed or are inactive, deactivated, deleted, or suspended.

You can also export a list containing the emails you imported, as well as the emails of users who subscribed to you before April 2025, when we rolled out changes to the email subscriptions. Export is only available on the web. If your list only includes users who subscribed after April 2025, the export will be empty.

The subscriber list is updated daily.

Email notifications

People can subscribe to your profile and receive your story directly in their email inbox when you publish.

Email your stories

You can decide whether to send notification of your story to your subscribers upon or after publishing. If you choose to do so, a short preview of the story will appear in the email, directing your subscribers to read more on Medium. It will take a few minutes to send the email, and once it’s sent, you’ll get a copy of it in your inbox. You can only send a story to your subscribers once.

How to send a story upon publishing it
  • On your homepage, click on your profile picture.
  • Click Write a story from the menu.
  • Write a story and click Publish. To learn more about publishing a story, see this article.
  • The option to Email a link to my subscribers will appear and be automatically selected on the right side of screen. If you don’t see this option, it means you don’t have any subscribers yet.
  • Click Publish and send now.
How to send a story after publishing it

  • Go to your story page.

  • Click the three-dot button button in the top-right corner of your story.

  • Click Email to subscribers. Please note:

  • If you published a paywalled story through the Partner Program, you will not be paid for any time spent reading the preview of the story in the email.

  • If you email your story to your subscribers, it will restrict some edit features after publishing, such as resizing images or embedding and unfurling new links in the story.

Allow reply to your story email

To help you build a 1-to-1 communication with your email subscribers, you can allow readers to reply to your story sent to their inbox.

  • On your homepage, click on your profile picture.
  • Click Settings from the menu.
  • In the Publishing tab, scroll down to Allow replies.
  • To enable it, click the checkbox. You can use a different email address by entering a new address in ‘Reply to’ email address.

Track and view your subscribers

You can track the growth of your mailing list and see who your subscribers are.

Track the growth of your mailing list:
  • On your homepage, click on your profile picture.
  • Click Stats from the menu.
  • Click on the Audience stats button in the top-right corner of the page.
View your subscribers:
  • On your homepage, click on your profile picture.
  • Click Stats from the menu.
  • Click on the Audience stats button in the top-right corner of the page.
  • Click View details within the Email subscribers section towards the top of the page. Learn more about your audience stats.

Import your subscribers

Writers can import a list of the subscribers you collect from elsewhere. You can only import by agreeing to follow our Email Import Terms of Use, including important requirements that apply to your use of the import tool. For example, you agree to only upload subscribers who’ve signed up to receive emails directly from you, and have not been obtained from a third party.

Before you start

Check your file for the following before you start your import:

  • The file format can be either in .CSV or .TXT. If the format is different, open the file in a spreadsheet application (ex. Google Sheets) and export it as a .CSV or .TXT file.
  • The file should not be bigger than 12MB and contain no more than 25,000 email addresses.
  • The only data Medium requires is the email address. Make sure the first line within the column containing email addresses includes the word email. See examples below.
Upload email lists
  • On your homepage, click on your profile picture.
  • Click Settings from the menu.
  • In the Publishing tab, click on Import email subscribers.
  • Review and confirm terms for import.
  • Click on Select contacts to upload the file.
  • Check your email. You’ll receive an import completion or retry message within 30 mins.
  • To verify your imported email addresses, click on the Take a look button in the email or click on View details in the Email subscribers section within Your audience stats to see the email addresses imported.
Troubleshooting

Why were some of my email addresses not imported?

Here are reasons that can prevent some emails from being added to your audience:

  • they have already subscribed to you
  • they have previously unsubscribed, blocked or muted your account
  • their accounts are suspended, deactivated or deleted How do I delete an imported email?

Please submit a request with the Medium support team here.

Why am I getting an error that says “That file isn’t formatted correctly”?

Try cleaning up your file. Remove all information except for email addresses and the column title, ‘email’. Find more details above in the “Before you start” section.

Why am I not allowed to import email addresses?

Depending on the account status, we may need you to submit additional info for quick verification with the Medium support team. Please send a request here.

Export your subscribers

NOTE: Email addresses are no longer shared with writers when readers subscribe to receive email notifications about stories from a writer. Writers can continue to export their existing email lists.

Writers can export a list of their subscribers. You can only export by agreeing to follow our terms, which include important requirements that apply to your use of subscriber information. For example, you agree not to sell this information or use it in ways that violate applicable laws or the rights of others.

  • On your homepage, click on your profile picture.
  • Click Settings from the menu.
  • Select the Publishing tab.
  • Click Export email subscribers.
  • Click Confirm export. Please note: The count of email subscribers shown at the top of the page is updated daily. However, the list of your email subscribers is updated in real-time, so the numbers may differ.

Stats

Medium’s stats give you insights into how your stories perform. This central dashboard provides analytics on your stories, audience, and earnings if you’re part of the Partner Program. Understanding these metrics will help you improve your content strategy and grow your audience on Medium.

Web App

  • To access your Stats, click on the menu icon in the top left corner to open the sidebar.
  • Click on Stats.
  • To access your Stats, tap on your profile picture in the bottom right corner.
  • Tap View Stats. From Stats, you can access specific tabs for Stories, Audience, and Partner Program earnings.

Stories

In Monthly, you can view an aggregate of the different stats for a specific month, along with a graph displaying the number of views and reads per day.

Monthly
  • Presentations: How many times Medium has suggested your stories to readers on the website or app. This includes all feeds, search results, profiles, publications, lists, as well as email and push notifications. The Digest is excluded. Presentations are not only available for stories published before 2025.
  • Views: How many times readers have accessed your stories. For a view to be counted, the reader has to stay on the story for at least 5 seconds, as we account for bouncing. If your stories have significant external traffic, views may be higher than presentations.
  • Reads: How many times readers have read your story for at least 30 seconds.
  • Followers: How many readers have followed your account.
  • Subscribers: How many readers have opted in to receive email notifications about your stories. All metrics are unique, meaning they are deduplicated. Presentations are deduplicated per user, surface, and day, while views and reads are deduplicated per user and day.

Monthly stats are updated hourly. To review previous months, click the pill-shaped select at the top right corner of this section and select the month you want to review.

Lifetime

The Lifetime section lists all your published stories along with some metrics: presentations (not available for stories published before 2025), views, reads, and earnings (only available for paywalled stories, marked with the star icon).

These are lifetime metrics, meaning they show the total number of presentations, views, and reads the story has received since it was published, and the total earnings accrued since and/or while it was paywalled.

The Lifetime section is updated hourly for stories during the first month after publishing. For stories published more than a month ago, this section is updated daily. The list of stories is sortable by views, reads, and lifetime earnings by clicking on the pill-shaped select on the top right corner of the section and selecting the preferred order option.

By clicking on the story, you can access the story’s detailed stats page to find additional details.

Audience

The Audience tab offers insights into your Medium community, showing who follows and engages with your content, including followers and email subscribers. It helps track audience growth and understanding over time.

Learn more about Audience stats.

Partner Program earnings

If you’ve been part of the Partner Program, you can access the Partner Program earnings dashboard, showing your earnings for eligible stories over a period. It includes sections: Overview, Earnings summary, Earnings by story, and a sidebar with your enrollment status, payout settings, rollover balance (if available), and quick links.

Learn more about the Partner Program earnings dashboard.

  • Publication stats
  • Frequently asked questions about Medium Stats

Story’s detailed stats page

Each story has its own detailed stats page where you can view comprehensive information about your story’s performance over time.

The first thing you will see is the story preview at the top of the page. If your story has been boosted or featured in a publication, you will also see a badge that indicates that.

Below the preview, the stats page is split into: Lifetime, Monthly, Traffic sources, Friend links (if the story is paywalled), and Audience interests.

Lifetime

The Reach funnel shows you how your story is performing since it was published.

  • Total number of presentations. How many times Medium has suggested your stories within the website or app, or through email and push notifications (excluding the Digest). Presentations are not available for stories published before 2025.

  • Total number of views. How many times readers have landed on your story. This section also includes the Feed clickthrough rate, which represents the percentage of views your story is getting in relation to presentations made exclusively via the feeds. Keep in mind that the Feed clickthrough rate will only be shown when there’s enough data.

  • Total number of reads. How many times readers have read your stories for at least 30 seconds. This section also includes the total Read ratio. Impact gives you additional performance metrics.

  • Earnings show you the total amount accrued by the story since it was paywalled. This section is only visible on paywalled stories.

  • Followers show the total number of readers who opted to follow or unfollow you after reading the story.

  • Subscribers show the total number of readers who opted to subscribe or unsubscribe after reading the story. The Lifetime metrics are updated hourly (except earnings, which are updated daily).

Monthly

The monthly section shows how readers are engaging with your story on a given month, which can be selected by clicking on the pill-shaped select on the top right corner of this section.

The included metrics are earnings, views, reads, and engagement. This last section includes:

  • Total clappers: The number of readers who clapped on this story at least once.
  • Total highlighters: The number of readers who highlighted this story at least once.
  • Total responders: The number of readers who wrote at least one response to this story.
  • Total followers: The number of readers who followed you after reading this story. It’s important to note that whenever you see yellow in the charts, that means the metric could be contributing to the story earnings. Keep in mind that stories only accrue earnings while they are paywalled and do not earn retroactively.

The Reads section also includes the Member read ratio that we use to adjust your earnings and discourage clickbait and misleading titles. This ratio is defined as: “member reads ÷ member views.” Learn more about the Partner Program earnings.

This section is updated daily.

Traffic sources

Here, you’ll see a breakdown of your traffic sources. You’ll see the percentage of views that came from Medium’s distribution—and then a list of your top external sources.

This section is updated hourly.

Friend links

If your story is paywalled, you’ll also see the number of views that came from your personal Friend link. Friend links are special links that you can use to give your readers free access to a story you publish behind our paywall.

This section is updated hourly.

Readers’ interests

To help you understand your audience, the Audience interests shows you a chart with the topics that the readers of the story are most interested in. The listed percentages are the proportion of logged-in viewers of the story who follow each topic. You can use this data to decide what you might want to write about next and to see whether you’re reaching your intended audience.

This section will populate if we can detect enough affinity for a particular topic from your audience.

That analysis is based on whether they have Medium accounts, the explicit interests of those accounts, and their reading history. If we don’t have enough data, it won’t populate.

So, for example, if you have a story with a large amount of external traffic, we won’t have any data on those readers, as they are likely not to be account holders.

If you have a lot of readers who are account holders and still don’t see that section, it is possible they are not explicitly following the topics you assigned to the story. It is also possible that those accounts don’t follow any topics, or we don’t have enough data to determine their interests.

This section is updated daily.

  • Publication stats

Managing your stories

Check your story’s paywall status

There are two ways to check whether a story is currently behind the paywall and eligible to earn.

Option 1: From the story page

Open the story you want to check and look for the member-only story badge with the star icon at the top of the page. If the icon is visible, the story is behind the paywall. This works the same on both web and app.

Option 2: From your profile

All your published stories are visible on your profile. Paywalled stories are marked with a star icon near the publishing date. This also works the same on both web and app.

Adding or removing a story from the paywall

  • Make a story eligible to earn money
  • Remove a story from the paywall

Content not eligible for the paywall

Not all content is eligible to earn in the Medium Partner Program. The following types of stories are not permitted behind the paywall. Stories found in violation of this policy will be removed from paywall eligibility.

Meta content about Medium

Stories primarily focused on Medium itself, the Partner Program, or the mechanics of the platform are not eligible for the paywall. This includes content about writing strategies on Medium, earnings, distribution, or other platform-specific topics.

Medium-focused stories are set to network-only distribution by default, as they are not of broad general interest to the wider member audience. Writing this type of content behind the paywall may also limit your long-term reach, as readers outside the Medium ecosystem are unlikely to engage with it.

You are welcome to write about Medium — including strategies, opinions, and critiques — outside the paywall.

Fully AI-generated writing

Stories where the majority of the content has been generated by an AI writing tool, with little or no human editing, fact-checking, or original contribution, are not eligible for the paywall.

This policy does not apply to the use of AI-assisted tools for outlining, spell-checking, grammar, or fact verification — only to content that is primarily AI-generated.

For more information, see the Artificial Intelligence (AI) content policy.

Make a story eligible to earn

As a Partner Program writer, you can choose whether each story earns money on a per-story basis. When a story is made eligible to earn, it is placed behind the paywall and becomes accessible only to Medium members.

You need to be enrolled in the Medium Partner Program to use this feature. See Medium Partner Program: Application and enrollment for more details.

You can set the paywall to be enabled or disabled by default for all new stories in your Publishing settings. The steps below let you manage the paywall setting for individual stories.

Publishing a new story behind the paywall

Web App

  • Once you’re done writing, click Publish.
  • Make sure the Paywall your story checkbox is selected.
  • Click Publish now.
  • Once you’re done writing your story, tap Next.
  • Enable the Members only toggle.
  • Tap Publish now.

Making a published story eligible to earn

Web App

  • Go to your story page, click the three-dot menu in the top-right corner, and select Edit story.
  • In edit mode, click the three-dot menu in the top-right corner again.
  • From the dropdown, select Manage paywall setting.
  • Choose the option to make the story eligible to earn.
  • Click Save.
  • Go to your story page, tap the three-dot menu in the top-right corner, and select Edit.
  • In edit mode, tap Next in the top-right corner to open the story settings.
  • Enable the Members only toggle.
  • Tap Publish to save your changes. Once your stories are behind the paywall, they will begin accruing earnings based on member activity: reading, listening, claps, highlights, and replies. For more details, see How Medium calculates earnings. You can track your earnings in your Partner Program dashboard.

Remove a story from the paywall

Partner Program writers can make any paywalled story freely accessible to all readers at any time. Follow the steps below to remove a story from the paywall.

Note: You must be an active Partner Program member to manage paywall settings.

Web App

  • Go to your story page, click the three-dot menu in the top-right corner, and select Edit story.
  • In edit mode, click the three-dot menu in the top-right corner again.
  • From the dropdown, select Manage paywall setting.
  • Choose the option to make the story not eligible to earn.
  • Click Save. The story will now be accessible to all readers.
  • Go to your story page, tap the three-dot menu in the top-right corner, and select Edit.
  • In edit mode, tap Next in the top-right corner to open the story settings.
  • Disable the Members only toggle.
  • Tap Publish to save your changes. The story will now be accessible to all readers.

How do I redeem a gift code in the Medium app?

There is no way to redeem your gift code directly in the Medium app. You can open the link medium.com/redeem in a web browser (either on a computer or mobile device) while logged in to your Medium account to redeem your code.

Do I need to connect a credit card to be able to redeem the code?

No. Redeeming a membership code activates a non-recurring membership plan for one year, and after that time it will cancel automatically unless you decide to renew it.

About me.dm, Medium’s Mastodon instance

Mastodon is a social media platform that’s different from the others: it’s not owned by any one entity. Mastodon is a network of servers (or “instances”) that are each hosted and moderated by their respective owners. Posts on Mastodon are federated, meaning that they have the potential to be seen and re-shared by anyone in the entire network. We think this presents an exciting new opportunity for writers to reach more readers. Learn more about our approach to Mastodon.

Learn more about Mastodon.

Visit Medium’s Mastodon instance, me.dm.

What you’ll get when you join me.dm

  • A fresh, short Mastodon username like @you@me.dm that matches your Medium profile.
  • An easy way to share your own Medium stories or short-form thoughts to millions of potential readers on the Mastodon network.
  • Access to a fast, stable, professionally-operated Mastodon instance.
  • A great local feed featuring posts from fellow Medium writers and readers.
  • Hand-picked recommendations for interesting people to follow on Mastodon.

Create your me.dm account

Desktop, iOS and Android

You must have a Medium account with an active membership subscription in order to create a me.dm account. Learn more about becoming a Medium member.

  • Go to medium.com/mastodon.
  • Confirm your username and create your me.dm password.
  • Click Create account. Your profile information on Medium will be copied over to your new profile on me.dm. You can edit your profile information at any time, but the username cannot be changed.

Learn more

  • For more information about managing your account, posting content, and using Mastodon, please visit the official Mastodon documentation.
  • Read our getting started list that can help you get oriented and make the most of Mastodon.
  • Follow our team account @medium.
